Energy needed to bring Q to the height of O Law: the kinetic energy is never negative, so the potential energy cannot exceed the constant energy Source: Notes.pdf, Eq. 4.28; Notes.pdf, Eq. 4.31
$$\begin{aligned} E_0 &= 0 - mg\ell\left(2\cos\tfrac{\pi}{4} + \cos\tfrac{\pi}{4}\right) = -\frac{3\sqrt2}{2}\,mg\ell = -2.121\,mg\ell \quad &\text{(at rest, $\theta = \phi = \pi/4$)} \\ y_Q &= -\ell\left(\cos\theta + \cos\phi\right) = 0 \iff \cos\phi = -\cos\theta \quad &\text{($Q$ on the axis $Ox$)} \\ V &= -mg\ell\left(2\cos\theta - \cos\theta\right) = -mg\ell\cos\theta \ge -mg\ell \quad &\text{(potential there; $\cos\theta \le 1$)} \\ E_0 - V &\le -2.121\,mg\ell + mg\ell = -1.121\,mg\ell \lt 0 \quad &\text{(the kinetic energy would be negative)} \end{aligned}$$
$$\boxed{E_0 = -\tfrac{3\sqrt2}{2}\,mg\ell \lt -mg\ell \le V\big|_{y_Q = 0}: \quad Q \ \text{never reaches, so never crosses, the axis } Ox}$$

Units: $[mg\ell] = [\mathrm{M\,L^2\,T^{-2}}]$

Meaning: whatever the configuration with $Q$ at the height of $O$, it needs at least the potential energy $-mg\ell$, more than the energy available; the motion stays below the axis.

(f) Small oscillations with the extra constraint

Energy of the constrained system for small angles Law: the extra constraint is ideal and stationary (no work), so the energy of part (d) is still conserved; second-order expansion in the small angle; comparison with the energy of a harmonic oscillator Source: Notes.pdf, Eq. 5.6; [Lecture 6, Slide 7]; [Lecture 3, Slide 16]; Problems.pdf, p. 35

With $\phi$ equal to $2\theta$ the system has one degree of freedom, $\theta$. The kinetic energy is already quadratic in the rate, so its coefficient is kept to zeroth order in $\theta$; the potential is expanded to second order with the Taylor series of the cosine.

$$\begin{aligned} \dot\phi &= 2\dot\theta \quad &\text{(derivative of the extra constraint)} \\ \cos\left(\theta - \phi\right) &= \cos\left(-\theta\right) = \cos\theta \quad &\text{(angle between the bars; cosine is even)} \\ K &= \tfrac12 m\ell^2\left[2 + 4 + 4\cos\theta\right]\dot\theta^2 \simeq \tfrac12 m\ell^2\left(10\right)\dot\theta^2 = 5m\ell^2\dot\theta^2 \quad &\text{(kinetic energy of part (d); $\cos\theta \simeq 1$)} \\ \cos\theta &\simeq 1 - \tfrac{\theta^2}{2} \quad &\text{(Taylor series to second order)} \\ \cos 2\theta &\simeq 1 - \tfrac{\left(2\theta\right)^2}{2} = 1 - 2\theta^2 \quad &\text{(the same series with the argument $2\theta$)} \\ V &= -mg\ell\left(2\cos\theta + \cos 2\theta\right) \simeq -mg\ell\left(2 - \theta^2 + 1 - 2\theta^2\right) = -3mg\ell + 3mg\ell\,\theta^2 \quad &\text{(potential of part (d))} \\ E + 3mg\ell &\simeq 5m\ell^2\dot\theta^2 + 3mg\ell\,\theta^2 = \tfrac12\left(10m\ell^2\right)\left(\dot\theta^2 + \frac{3g}{5\ell}\,\theta^2\right) \quad &\text{(common factor $5m\ell^2$)} \\ 10m\ell^2\dot\theta\ddot\theta + 6mg\ell\,\theta\dot\theta &= 0 \Rightarrow \ddot\theta + \frac{3g}{5\ell}\,\theta = 0 \quad &\text{(time derivative of the constant energy, chain rule; divided by $10m\ell^2\dot\theta$)} \end{aligned}$$
$$\boxed{\omega_0 = \sqrt{\frac{3g}{5\ell}}}$$

Units: $[g/\ell] = [\mathrm{T^{-2}}]$, so $[\omega_0] = [\mathrm{T^{-1}}]$

Meaning: the energy has the form of the hint, with the mass replaced by $10m\ell^2$ and the coordinate by $\theta$; the linearised equation is the harmonic oscillator of Notes.pdf, Eq. 5.6. The frequency squared is 0.6 times that of a simple pendulum of length $\ell$: $Q$, which moves three times as far as $P$, adds more inertia than restoring weight.

Part of the statementAnswer
(a) Kinematics$\mathbf r_0^P = \ell\,\mathbf i_1$, $\mathbf v_0^P = \ell\dot\theta\,\mathbf j_1$, $\mathbf a_0^P = \ell(\ddot\theta\,\mathbf j_1 - \dot\theta^2\,\mathbf i_1)$; $\mathbf r_0^Q = \ell(\mathbf i_1 + \mathbf i_2)$, $\mathbf v_0^Q = \ell(\dot\theta\,\mathbf j_1 + \dot\phi\,\mathbf j_2)$, $\mathbf a_0^Q = \ell(\ddot\theta\,\mathbf j_1 - \dot\theta^2\,\mathbf i_1 + \ddot\phi\,\mathbf j_2 - \dot\phi^2\,\mathbf i_2)$, with $\mathbf i_1 = \sin\theta\,\mathbf i - \cos\theta\,\mathbf j$, $\mathbf j_1 = \cos\theta\,\mathbf i + \sin\theta\,\mathbf j$ and the same with $\phi$
(b) Forces$P$: $-mg\,\mathbf j$, $-T_1\,\mathbf i_1$, $T_2\,\mathbf i_2$; $Q$: $-mg\,\mathbf j$, $-T_2\,\mathbf i_2$
(c) Equations of motion$2\ddot\theta + \ddot\phi\cos(\theta - \phi) + \dot\phi^2\sin(\theta - \phi) + (2g/\ell)\sin\theta = 0$; $\ddot\phi + \ddot\theta\cos(\theta - \phi) - \dot\theta^2\sin(\theta - \phi) + (g/\ell)\sin\phi = 0$
(d) Conservation law$\frac12 m\ell^2[2\dot\theta^2 + \dot\phi^2 + 2\dot\theta\dot\phi\cos(\theta - \phi)] - mg\ell(2\cos\theta + \cos\phi) = E$
(e) Crossing $Ox$never: $E_0 = -\frac{3\sqrt2}{2}mg\ell$ is below the least potential $-mg\ell$ with $Q$ on the axis
(f) Constrained small oscillations$\omega_0 = \sqrt{3g/(5\ell)}$

Check against the official key: Problems.pdf, p. 76 lists item 50 with no answer, so there is no key to compare with. Independent checks: the energy of part (d) was obtained from the powers of the forces, and its time derivative vanishes along the equations of part (c) (box below); the static forces of the bars are the expected ones; and the frequency of part (f) belongs to a family of constraints whose limits are checked below.

The energy is a first integral of the equations of motion Law: time derivative of the energy of part (d) (product and chain rules), compared with the equations of part (c) multiplied by the rates Source: Notes.pdf, Eq. 4.29; Notes.pdf, Eq. 4.31; Notes.pdf, Eq. 4.4

Write $\mathcal E_1$ and $\mathcal E_2$ for the left-hand sides of the first and second equations of the box of part (c); both vanish along every motion.

$$\begin{aligned} \frac{d}{dt}\cos\left(\theta - \phi\right) &= -\sin\left(\theta - \phi\right)\left(\dot\theta - \dot\phi\right) \quad &\text{(chain rule)} \\ \dot E &= m\ell^2\left[2\dot\theta\ddot\theta + \dot\phi\ddot\phi + \ddot\theta\dot\phi\cos\left(\theta - \phi\right) + \dot\theta\ddot\phi\cos\left(\theta - \phi\right) - \dot\theta\dot\phi\left(\dot\theta - \dot\phi\right)\sin\left(\theta - \phi\right)\right] + mg\ell\left(2\dot\theta\sin\theta + \dot\phi\sin\phi\right) \quad &\text{(product and chain rules on each term of $E$)} \\ m\ell^2\dot\theta\,\mathcal E_1 &= m\ell^2\left[2\dot\theta\ddot\theta + \dot\theta\ddot\phi\cos\left(\theta - \phi\right) + \dot\theta\dot\phi^2\sin\left(\theta - \phi\right)\right] + 2mg\ell\,\dot\theta\sin\theta \quad &\text{(first equation of part (c) times $m\ell^2\dot\theta$)} \\ m\ell^2\dot\phi\,\mathcal E_2 &= m\ell^2\left[\dot\phi\ddot\phi + \dot\phi\ddot\theta\cos\left(\theta - \phi\right) - \dot\theta^2\dot\phi\sin\left(\theta - \phi\right)\right] + mg\ell\,\dot\phi\sin\phi \quad &\text{(second equation of part (c) times $m\ell^2\dot\phi$)} \\ \dot E &= m\ell^2\dot\theta\,\mathcal E_1 + m\ell^2\dot\phi\,\mathcal E_2 \quad &\text{(term by term, with $-\dot\theta\dot\phi\left(\dot\theta - \dot\phi\right) = \dot\theta\dot\phi^2 - \dot\theta^2\dot\phi$)} \\ &= 0 \quad &\text{($\mathcal E_1 = \mathcal E_2 = 0$ along the motion)} \end{aligned}$$
$$\boxed{\dot E = m\ell^2\left(\dot\theta\,\mathcal E_1 + \dot\phi\,\mathcal E_2\right) = 0}$$

Units: $[m\ell^2\dot\theta\ddot\theta] = [mg\ell\dot\theta] = [\mathrm{M\,L^2\,T^{-3}}]$, a power

Meaning: the energy of part (d), found from the powers of the forces, is also a first integral of the equations of part (c), found by projections: the two routes agree, which checks the signs and coefficients of both.

Limiting cases: a family of imposed constraints Law: the energy method of part (f) with the constraint that $\phi$ equals $\lambda\theta$, for a constant $\lambda$ Source: Notes.pdf, Eq. 5.6; Notes.pdf, Sec. 4.7
$$\begin{aligned} K &\simeq \tfrac12 m\ell^2\left(2 + \lambda^2 + 2\lambda\right)\dot\theta^2 \quad &\text{(kinetic energy of part (d), $\dot\phi = \lambda\dot\theta$, $\cos(\theta - \phi) \simeq 1$)} \\ V &\simeq -3mg\ell + \tfrac12 mg\ell\left(2 + \lambda^2\right)\theta^2 \quad &\text{(Taylor series of $\cos\theta$ and $\cos\lambda\theta$)} \\ \omega_0^2 &= \frac{g}{\ell}\,\frac{2 + \lambda^2}{2 + 2\lambda + \lambda^2} \quad &\text{(ratio of the two coefficients)} \\ \lambda = 0:\ \omega_0^2 &= \frac{g}{\ell}\,\frac{2}{2} = \frac{g}{\ell} \quad &\text{(lower bar held vertical)} \\ \lambda = 2:\ \omega_0^2 &= \frac{g}{\ell}\,\frac{2 + 4}{2 + 4 + 4} = \frac{3g}{5\ell} \quad &\text{(the constraint of the statement)} \end{aligned}$$
$$\boxed{\lambda = 0:\ \omega_0 = \sqrt{g/\ell}\ \text{(simple pendulum)}; \qquad \lambda = 2:\ \omega_0 = \sqrt{3g/(5\ell)}\ \text{(part (f))}}$$

Units: $[\mathrm{T^{-2}}]$

Meaning: with the lower bar kept vertical both particles move with $P$ and swing together as one simple pendulum of length $\ell$, the frequency of Notes.pdf, Sec. 4.7; the general formula reproduces the result of part (f) for the constraint of the statement.
